## Audit additions (2026-10-21)

- [x] The frameless main window resizes from invisible edges and corners with the native resize cursors, as Prism Terminal's does (and its sizes: Electron's frameless zones are 5 px edges and 16 px corners, both inside the window), and Aero Snap by drag and Win+arrow still work (the Windows 11 snap layouts hover menu on Maximize does not exist in Flare; answering HTMAXBUTTON is a follow-up): a window subclass answers `WM_NCHITTEST` with `HTLEFT` to `HTBOTTOMRIGHT` inside an edge band of `round(5 * dpi / 96)` device px (5 at 100 percent, 11 at 225 percent, 15 at 300) with corners `round(16 * dpi / 96)` long, at the window's own DPI read on every hit test (so a monitor move needs no work), and nothing while maximized, minimized or full screen. The window buttons (the Close button's right 2 px no longer resize at 100 percent), the title-bar drag and the terminal are untouched, and no frame is drawn (DIFF-1 holds). The minimum is 560 x 400 (Prism Terminal's), and a programmatic resize below it is held to it. Scope of the checks: the sizes at 100, 150, 225 and 300 percent (edge 5, 8, 11, 15 px; corner 16, 24, 36, 48 px, literals) are held by the unit tests; the e2e reads the band through the real window procedure at the HOST MONITOR's dpi only (a simulated DPI step moves the app's scale, not the window's), and probes the Close button's last pixel (w - round(6 * scale) - 1, mid-bar) as client at the step whose scale is the window's. Rounding, checked against Chromium 2026-10-21 (`DesktopWindowTreeHostWin::GetNonClientComponent` maps the point with `ScreenWin::ClientToDIPPoint`, a floored scale, then Electron's `FramelessView` tests it against 5 and 16 DIP): PT's left and top band is `ceil(5 * s)` device px and its right and bottom depends on the window size in DIP, so at fractional scales (125 and 225 percent, say 7 and 12 px against Flare's 6 and 11) PT's band can be one pixel wider. They are identical at 100, 200 and 300 percent. DECISION: Flare keeps `round`, a one pixel difference at some fractional scales; not PT-exact there. The dictation widget and the popups are not resizable. (Owner bug 2026-10-21, "i cant adjust window size of flare when its not maximized theres no handles": winit answers `WM_NCCALCSIZE` with the whole window as client, so `DefWindowProc` hit-tested `HTCLIENT` everywhere. Audit: each_edge_answers_its_own_code, each_corner_answers_its_own_code_and_reaches_further_than_an_edge, the_band_scales_with_the_dpi_from_100_to_300_percent, a_flat_window_has_no_band, the_subclass_on_a_real_window_answers_ht_codes_at_the_edges_and_corners, a_programmatic_resize_on_a_real_window_is_clamped, crates/flare-win/src/resize_edges.rs; e2e resizeEdges, tools/e2e/src/scenarios/dpi.rs)
- [x] The Settings > Appearance theme wall is compact: columns = floor((wall width + 12) / (172 + 12)) with a 12 px gap, each card stretched to fill the row (never under 172 px less one device pixel, since the right edge is floored to a whole device pixel; the same height as before, 124 px plus two 1-device-pixel hairlines), and every card edge on a whole device pixel, so the strip right of the last card of a row is 0 to 2 css px and a wall wide enough for four at 172 shows four, at 100 to 300 percent and across monitor moves (the layout is recomputed from the window's scale at each rebuild). The Show all fold and the two-row clip are unchanged. (Owner 2026-10-21, screenshot at 225 percent: 3 cards per row with an empty strip nearly a fourth card wide; Prism Terminal PR #251: `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(172px, 1fr))`. Audit: layout_wraps_at_the_column_count, a_width_that_fits_four_at_172_gives_four_and_one_less_gives_three, the_cards_fill_the_row_on_whole_device_pixels_at_every_width_and_scale, a_partial_last_row_keeps_the_same_columns, crates/flare-settings/src/wall.rs; the_wall_cards_are_one_height_in_rows_of_the_right_count, crates/flare/src/ui_tests/settings_look.rs; e2e themeCards, tools/e2e/src/scenarios/settings_d.rs: sizes the window so the wall is 724 css px or more, reads the column count and strip from the layout, and reads the last card's right edge and the strip from the screenshot's device pixels). Edges are whole device pixels relative to the wall's own origin. DECISION (2026-10-21 audit): the editor's preview card (custom-preview) stays 196 px wide. After PT #251 its wrapper is `relative min-w-0` with a w-full button and no width of its own, so in PT it shrinks to its content (about 125 px, estimated from the 10.5 px mono lines, not measured: PT is not run from here); #251 does not mention it and its tests do not cover it, so it reads as a side effect. Flare keeps the 196 px preview (w=0 falls back to CARD_W). Revisit if the owner wants the narrow preview.
- [x] Every Settings row takes effect on the live window while Settings stays open, as in Prism Terminal: Tab position moves the tabs down the side or back along the top, the look rows (theme, background, secondary and accent colours, see through, Panel edges) are worn, the terminal font and its size reach the grid, the marks follow their switches and colours, Count marked tabs on the taskbar sets or clears the overlay, Command help goes or comes, Agent hotkeys, Keep agent sessions open and Tab switching hold for the next key, Detailed logging and Mark a problem act on the log, and the Dictation rows reach the dictation controller (it listens to its own keys). Rows Prism Terminal applies later stay that way: Default shell and Exact status from Claude Code (terminals opened after the change), Folder for new tabs (the next new tab). (Owner bug 2026-10-21, "if i go from vertical to horizontal tabs it doesnt change before i click out of settings": the tab view read the tab position and the taskbar count only on its next refresh, and only closing Settings caused one; AppCtl now listens to every row key the look, the help panel and dictation do not own, one PrefsChanged and one refresh per turn, and the terminal font follows the controller's own look listener. Audit: the_tab_position_moves_the_tabs_while_settings_is_open, the_look_rows_change_the_window_while_settings_is_open, the_terminal_font_rows_reach_the_grid_while_settings_is_open, the_key_rows_hold_for_the_next_key_while_settings_is_open, command_help_goes_while_settings_is_open, the_diagnostics_rows_act_while_settings_is_open, every_other_row_key_reaches_the_main_window, crates/flare/src/ui_tests/settings_live.rs, each at 100 to 300 percent; the_mark_rows_and_the_taskbar_count_apply_while_settings_is_open, crates/flare/src/ui_tests/agent_flows.rs; e2e settingsLive, tools/e2e/src/scenarios/settings_live.rs: Tab position both ways and Panel edges picked by the pointer, the sidebar, the strip and the line token read from the main window with Settings in front, at 100 percent and again after a move to a 225 percent monitor; e2e dictation switches dictation on from the page and sees the engine start with the page in front)
- [x] A path a program breaks at the window's edge by hand is one link, as in Prism Terminal core 0.44.2 (PT #250): Claude Code cuts its own rows at the width and writes each on its own, so the next row is not marked wrapped. Row y carries on row y-1 when the last cell of y-1 and the first of y both hold a path character (not white space, a quote, <>|*? or box drawing U+2500-U+257F) and the run across the edge holds \ or /; the logical line is then read across such rows with the join offsets (`cells::by_hand`, `logical_line_in`), the path ACROSS each join is tried first and, when it exists, covers its pieces, and when it names nothing the pieces are read as before (`path_candidates_in`, `PathLinks::paths_found`, `link_ranges_in`). A web link is never carried across a join (`find_links_in`). Wired into the link scan on both screens (each row keeps its own piece of the mark), so the paint, the hover, the click and the right-click menu all see one link; the viewport window extends over such rows too (`TermBuf::window_in`). Only paths are read across: an OSC 8 label cut that way stays one mark per row (a run stops at a join, like the stream spans Prism Terminal paints from), a sentence that ends at the edge is untouched, and find, selection and the caret still use the wrapped line alone. (Owner, 2026-10-21, via the Prism Terminal session: a long `C:\...\scrat` + `chpad\...\Flare-win-Setup.exe` was two pieces and no link. Audit: a_path_cut_at_the_edge_carries_on_into_the_next_row, no_hand_break_when_short_blank_or_no_separator, crates/flare-term/src/cells.rs; find_links_in_never_crosses_a_hand_break, crates/flare-term/src/links.rs; a_path_broken_by_hand_is_one_link_over_both_rows, a_path_ending_at_the_edge_keeps_its_link_when_the_joined_text_names_nothing, a_web_link_is_never_carried_across_a_hand_break, crates/flare-term/src/path_links.rs; a_path_broken_by_hand_is_one_mark_with_a_piece_per_row, an_osc8_label_cut_by_hand_is_a_mark_per_row, crates/flare-term/src/link_scan.rs; a_path_broken_by_hand_at_the_edge_is_one_link, a_path_broken_by_hand_on_the_alternate_screen_inks_each_row, a_web_link_is_never_carried_across_a_row_broken_by_hand, crates/flare-term/src/emulator.rs; e2e pathLinks, tools/e2e/src/scenarios/input_b.rs `path_cut_at_the_edge`: pwsh writes a row exactly the window's width then the rest of an existing absolute path, both rows ink as one path mark, and a click on the second row opens the whole file through the e2e seam)
- [x] The e2e judges a breathing mark only in a picture drawn at full breath: the question dot (Ring) fades from full to nothing and back every 2 s, so a ring-ink check on whichever moment a snapshot caught read 0.28 of the ink once and failed (gate run 2026-10-21, `indicatorStyles` "volt, ring: every ring wears its name's ink", passing alone). The app now reads `MarkMotion.breathe` (1.0 when still) on the UI thread right after each snapshot, as it does the sidebar slide (audit V14), and `frames.series` reports it as `snap_breathe`; the ring checks of `indicatorStyles` and `tabLooks` wait for a picture at breath 0.85 or more (it comes round once per 2 s breath) and check the same thresholds as before. The app's drawing is unchanged. (Audit: the_snapshot_reports_the_breath_its_picture_shows, crates/flare/src/e2e_glue.rs; e2e indicatorStyles "a picture of the question dot at full breath", tools/e2e/src/scenarios/indicators.rs; e2e tabLooks, `snap_full_breath` in tools/e2e/src/scenarios/strip.rs)

- [x] Powerline symbols U+E0B0 to U+E0BF are drawn by Flare itself, cell-exact in both renderers (owner bug 2026-10-21: Claude Code's status line, with U+E0B1, U+E0B4 and U+E0B6, showed a tofu box and a star). Root cause, confirmed by shaping each character: the stack's first face, Cascadia Mono, has no Powerline glyphs, so cosmic-text's own fallback answered them with Segoe UI Symbol, which maps those private-use codepoints to unrelated drawings (glyph ids 4856 to 4862). What Prism Terminal does: its terminal is xterm.js 6.0 with the DOM renderer (no WebGL or canvas addon is installed, and xterm 6.0's core has no `CustomGlyphs` code; that file belongs to the WebGL addon), so PT draws nothing itself: Chromium takes each character from the CSS stack in order (`"Cascadia Mono", "JetBrainsMono NF", Consolas`), and the pills the owner sees come from JetBrainsMono NF. Flare's old behaviour (first family only, then a system fallback list) differed. Now: (1) `crates/flare-render/src/shapes.rs` draws U+E0B0 to U+E0BF (solid and thin arrows, solid and outline half circles, the corner triangles and diagonals) as an anti-aliased coverage mask exactly the cell's width and height at the cell's baseline, tinted with the cell's foreground by both painters like a font glyph, so a cap's flat side meets the neighbouring background with no seam at any scale (stroke width is the scale, at least one device pixel); it is hooked in `Rasterizer::rasterize` ahead of any font, the same glyph-source path both painters already use, so there is no second path. Box drawing U+2500 to U+257F, blocks and shades U+2580 to U+259F are still the font's (Cascadia Mono has them, as PT's Chromium takes them from the same face); they are not redrawn. (2) Any other private-use character (U+E000 to U+F8FF and the supplementary planes) is looked up only in the stack's own families in order, as PT's CSS stack does, so JetBrainsMono NF icons show; a private-use character no stack family has stays blank and is never answered by Segoe UI Symbol or another fallback font (`rasterize_private_use`). Unverified difference: Chromium may show its own notdef box or a system font's glyph for such a character; Flare shows nothing. Held by shapes.rs unit tests (every shape fills the cell box at 100, 150, 200, 225 and 300 percent; the cap's flat column is solid and the two caps mirror; arrow, chevron, outline and triangle shapes), raster.rs `powerline_shapes_are_drawn_to_the_cell_at_every_scale`, `the_statusline_pill_has_no_seam_between_cap_and_bar`, `a_private_use_character_is_never_a_symbol_fonts_unrelated_glyph`, `box_drawing_and_shades_still_come_from_the_font`, gpu_parity `the_statusline_pill_paints_the_same_on_both_painters`, and e2e `powerlineGlyphs` (chevron on red, left cap, bar cell, right cap: the shapes' pixels in the cell colours, the chevron a stroke and not a box, no seam, both renderers; tools/e2e/src/scenarios/input_b.rs). Differences from PT, deliberate: PT (xterm.js 6.0.0, DOM renderer, no customGlyphs) takes these characters from its CSS font stack and, where no stack family has one, from Chromium's system fallback (Segoe UI Symbol: a star or a box), so Flare's shapes are an improvement on every stack, not a copy. For any other private-use character (Nerd Font icons, U+E0A0 and so on) missing from every stack family Flare draws nothing where PT would show that unrelated fallback glyph; not verified by rendering in PT. A shape cell with a trailing variation selector or combining mark is still the shape. Switching the font stack loads any installed family of the new stack (a Nerd Font) at once.

## Dialogs and right-click (2026-10-22)

Compared with Prism Terminal 0.58.3 (origin/main 60b6a4a): `Modal.tsx`, `Dialog.tsx`, `UpdateDialog.tsx`,
`ThemeSwitchAsk.tsx`, `ModelDownloadAsk.tsx`, `agentClose.ts`, `App.tsx` (the close question).

- [x] DIFF-fixed: the close question's confirm is the solid accent with the on-accent ink; no danger fill (Flare passed `danger: true` from v0.55.6). Text, 420 px box, button order and labels unchanged and equal to PT's. (`a_tab_hosting_an_agent_asks_naming_it_and_working_for`)
- [x] DIFF-fixed: focus ring is `focus-visible` only: the close question (`dialog.slint`), the update window (`update.slint`) and the theme-switch question (`wall.slint` AskThree) draw no ring after a click and draw it after Tab, Enter or (close question) a chord that opened it. The close question knows a Ctrl+W/close chord opened it (`dialog-by-key`); other doors (window close, install) count as pointer. (`the_ring_shows_only_after_the_keyboard`, `a_dialog_opened_by_a_chord_shows_the_ring_from_the_start`)
- [x] Same as PT, checked: the model download question (`DictAsk`) uses the neutral row buttons and the existing key-only focus edge; no shadow on the two row-button questions; Escape and a press outside cancel; Tab stays inside.
- [x] Not needed: PT's `returnsFocus` rule (core 0.34.2: focus goes back on close only when it went down with the dialog) guards a DOM focus hand-back. Flare's dialogs are Slint overlays over one focus scope, and the front terminal takes the focus when a question closes (`focus_returns_to_the_terminal_after_close`), so there is no second owner to protect.
- [x] DIFF-22 (deliberate): a right-click never reaches a program unless Settings > Terminal > "Send right clicks to programs" is on (default off). PT (xterm.js) forwards a right press to a program that asked for the mouse AND opens its menu, so a right-click in Claude Code can paste there; the owner asked for this not to happen (2026-10-22). With the setting on, Flare behaves as before and as PT does.

- [x] A Finished or Failed mark held on the front tab while the window was away clears only when the front tab changes to a DIFFERENT tab (owner, 2026-10-23: "it should only disappear once I click a new tab"). Clicking the tab already in front, focus returning and the pointer moving keep it; closing another tab keeps it; background-tab marks are unchanged. NARROWED from "a click on any tab" (the 2026-10-21 rule, PT core 0.44.4): PT #252 is being narrowed the same way by the Prism Terminal session, so the two are to match once it lands (check agentHold.ts against `Indicator::set_looking`). (crates/flare-agent/src/indicator.rs, a_click_on_the_same_tab_keeps_a_held_mark, a_click_on_another_tab_releases_the_mark_on_the_tab_left; crates/flare/src/appctl.rs, a_pick_of_the_same_tab_keeps_a_held_mark; e2e agentHooks)

## macOS port, phase 2: the terminal on the Mac (2026-11-08)

The M2 and M5 terminal lines above, as they now hold on macOS (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-macos-port.md`,
phase 2; regression rules 227 and 1001 to 1004). Each line names the Windows behaviour it stands in for and the macOS tests
that hold it. Windows behaviour is unchanged.

- [x] Shells: zsh, bash, fish and pwsh are found by absolute path (the login shell from the user record first, then Homebrew, `/etc/shells`, `/bin`), never through PATH; the login shell is listed first and is the default. Stands in for the pwsh, powershell, cmd and WSL list. (macOS: a_stock_mac_lists_zsh_then_bash, the_login_shell_comes_first_and_is_the_default, homebrew_wins_over_the_system_copy_unless_the_login_shell_says_otherwise, pwsh_is_found_on_homebrew_or_the_package_path, shell_by_id_falls_back_to_the_first_on_macos, crates/flare-shells/src/macos/detect.rs and list.rs)
- [x] Every shell is a login shell with the login PATH (`/etc/paths` through path_helper) even when Flare starts from launchd, and the user's dotfiles are never written: zsh through the `ZDOTDIR` shim, bash through `--rcfile`, fish through `--init-command`, pwsh with `-Login`. Bash tabs start without Apple's "default interactive shell is now zsh" notice. (macOS: every_shell_starts_as_a_login_shell, *_starts_as_a_login_shell_with_flares_variables, zsh_reads_the_users_startup_files_from_their_own_zdotdir, bash_reads_the_users_login_files, the_hooks_never_write_the_users_dotfiles, bash_starts_without_apples_zsh_notice_and_a_users_choice_is_kept)
- [x] The pty environment: the shared `pty_env` rules (TERM, COLORTERM, FLARE_TAB_ID, the removed session markers) plus `TERM_PROGRAM=Flare`, its version, and `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` when no locale is set. (macOS: names_flare_as_the_terminal_and_drops_the_launchers, a_missing_locale_becomes_utf8_and_a_set_one_is_kept, sh_starts_answers_and_its_environment_is_exactly_what_was_given)
- [x] Folder report: OSC 7 replaces OSC 9;9 and the cmd PROMPT; Flare's hooks send `file:///path` with no host, so a Mac renamed mid-session still counts; another machine's report is never followed; the exit status survives the hook. (macOS: *_reports_its_folder_and_follows_cd, the_bash_and_zsh_hooks_keep_the_exit_status, the_hooks_report_the_folder_with_no_host_so_a_renamed_mac_still_counts, osc_7_is_the_folder_report_on_macos, a_remote_report_never_replaces_the_local_folder_on_macos, crates/flare-shells/src/macos/osc7.rs)
- [x] decideFollow and cdCommand: POSIX folders, case kept, `/` only; the cd line is `cd -- '<path>'` for zsh and bash and `cd '<path>'` in fish quoting. (macOS: decide_follow_tells_same_inside_outside, posix_shells_get_a_quoted_cd_that_ends_options)
- [x] Pty spawn, resize, exit code and the foreground job (`tcgetpgrp`): the real shell answers, `stty size` follows a resize, an exit is reported with its code. (macOS: pty::macos::tests::real, the_foreground_says_whether_a_program_runs_in_the_tab, a_shell_that_exits_by_itself_is_reported_and_removed)
- [x] Closing a tab ends its whole session (SIGHUP, then SIGTERM and SIGKILL after 250 ms and 500 ms), its jobs, orphans and HUP-ignoring children, sparing an `.app` bundle; the Windows kill-on-close job object's twin. Known gaps (rule 1001): a daemon that double-forks with `setsid` before any round sees it, and a job that ignores SIGHUP when Flare crashes. (macOS: closing_the_tab_ends_the_shell_its_jobs_orphans_and_stubborn_children, a_crash_of_the_terminal_hangs_up_its_shells, a_member_seen_once_is_followed_out_of_the_session_but_never_a_recycled_number)
- [x] Quit waits for every killed shell with a 3 s cap, spares included, and on macOS also ends what the shells left behind before Flare exits. (macOS: ten_real_shells_with_jobs_killed_none_remain, kill_while_output_flows_50_times, a_warm_spare_is_killed_at_quit, quitting_ends_a_job_that_ignores_the_hangup_before_the_process_exits)
- [x] Path links and the stat meter: the POSIX grammar (`/`, `~/`, `file://` URLs on this machine, `\ ` escaped spaces); nothing under an automount point (`/net`, `/Network`, `/home`, their `/System/Volumes/Data` forms, any `autofs` mount) or a `//` path is ever statted, restored, adopted or read in a panel, through one shared check. (macOS: find_paths_knows_posix_paths, a_posix_path_in_a_status_line_stops_at_the_prompt, an_automount_path_is_never_resolved, every_automount_point_is_refused_in_every_spelling, a_dot_dot_path_to_an_automount_is_never_loadable, only_a_local_file_of_the_right_kind_is_loadable_posix, restore_never_spawns_in_a_remote_folder_on_macos)
- [x] Fonts: the stack falls to SF Mono and Menlo in Consolas' place, with the macOS fallback families; the rasterizer tests run on the Mac. (macOS: crates/flare-render/src/raster.rs tests, crates/flare-render/tests/metal_macos.rs)

## macOS port, phase 3: the Mac chrome (2026-11-08)

The M1 and M3 window lines above, as they hold on macOS (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-macos-port.md`,
phase 3; regression rule 1005). Max chose Mac-native chrome: traffic lights, the Mac menu bar, Cmd shortcuts, Mac
glass, the same Flare content inside.

- [x] Native window: titled, transparent titlebar, hidden title, full-size content; the traffic lights sit in Flare's 36 pt top row in place of the mark, and Flare's own minimise, maximise and close are left out (the Settings cog and Command help stay). Stands in for the frameless DWM window and its caption buttons. (macOS: lights_fit_the_top_row_and_stay_there_after_a_resize, top_row_reaches_flare_everywhere_but_on_the_lights, crates/flare-win/tests/appkit.rs; a_mac_window_keeps_its_native_frame, crates/flare/src/backend.rs)
- [x] Glass: an `NSVisualEffectView` behind the content (Acrylic as the under-window background), light or dark by the ground; the material state machine and its order (D7) are the Windows ones. (macOS: glass_sits_behind_the_content_and_changes_in_place, frost_follows_the_grounds_light_or_dark, acrylic_is_the_strong_blur_and_none_takes_the_glass_away)
- [x] Resize edges, full screen and zoom are AppKit's; the window menu (Alt+Space) is not applicable (the Mac menu bar holds those commands); a titlebar double click follows System Settings. (macOS: the_double_click_follows_the_users_setting, crates/flare-win/src/macos/chrome.rs; resize_edges.rs and sysmenu.rs say why they install nothing)
- [x] The window remembers its frame: the screens' visible frames, all in one space (points times the saved window's scale, as winit reports a position), check a saved place, so screens of different scales never overlap (rule 1011). (macOS: screens_are_device_pixel_areas, a_screen_becomes_a_top_left_device_pixel_area, screens_of_different_scales_never_overlap, the_scale_is_the_one_of_the_screen_under_the_point, crates/flare-win/src/macos/chrome.rs; the_areas_are_asked_for_in_the_saved_scale, crates/flare-win/src/window_state.rs)
- [x] Session end: Quit, log out and shut down flush the tabs, the window and the settings; the app's end runs once, a power-off notice flushes every time and a cancelled log out leaves the final save armed (rule 1008); sleep, wake and the screens reach the watchdog. (macOS: the_end_runs_once_a_power_off_never_disarms_it_and_the_power_events_arrive_until_the_watch_stops, crates/flare-win/src/macos/session_end.rs)
- [x] The `--e2e` window parks off every screen in an accessory app (no Dock icon, never active), and the app is an accessory that does not activate from the event loop's first turn, before `show()` (rule 1010). (macOS: e2e_window_parks_off_every_screen_as_an_accessory, a_parked_accessory_window_has_no_problems, a_mac_e2e_run_is_an_accessory_that_never_activates_from_its_first_event, crates/flare/src/backend.rs)

A key item is its key press, through the typed key's path; Quit is the window's close route. - [x] The Cmd keymap: on macOS Cmd takes Ctrl's place for every app chord (one table, `KeyContext::os`), Ctrl+letter always reaches the shell as a control character, Ctrl+Tab still steps the tabs, the agent guard stays on Ctrl+C and Ctrl+D, Alt+Space is Windows only, Cmd+F finds even over a full-screen program; Windows keys are unchanged. - [x] Mac terminal keys: Cmd+C copies a selection, Cmd+V pastes, Cmd+A selects all, Ctrl+C is the interrupt even over a selection; a Cmd key that is no shortcut never types; Cmd+Left and Right are line start and end, Cmd+Backspace deletes to the line start, Option+Left and Right jump a word, Option+Backspace and Delete delete one; Option types the layout's character unless "Use Option as Meta" (`flare.term.optionAsMeta`, default off) is on. - [x] Trackpad scrolling is Terminal.app's: the view moves line for line while the fingers move and stops when they lift (AppKit's momentum events are dropped, for scrollback, the alternate screen and mouse-reporting programs alike); a mouse wheel notch is three lines as on Windows; a web panel keeps its own momentum (rule 1129). Windows is unchanged. - [x] Clipboard: `NSPasteboard` for text (exact UTF-8, emoji and CJK), copied files (POSIX paths, Finder's order) and an image seen, so the paste decision (files, then image key, then text) is the Windows one; the change count is the sequence. - [x] File drop on the window and the terminal: POSIX paths quoted for the tab's shell (zsh and bash POSIX quotes, fish its own, tcsh and csh POSIX quotes with `!` escaped), joined by one space, never Enter; a name with a control character types nothing. (macOS: a_mac_drop_is_quoted_for_zsh_bash_and_fish, crates/flare/src/drop_flow.rs; mac_shell_ids_read_as_the_language_they_speak, fish_quotes_escape_only_the_backslash_and_the_quote, csh_quotes_escape_the_history_bang_inside_the_single_quotes, crates/flare-shells/src/paste.rs)
- [x] Openers: web links through `NSWorkspace` (http and https with a host only), files in their default app after the allowlist, and only when that app cannot run them (no terminal, script runner or unknown app, no execute bit; opened with the checked app, rule 1009), folders and Settings' "Open folder" only ever shown in Finder (a bundle is shown, never launched), reveal selects in Finder. (macOS: only_http_and_https_with_a_host_pass, a_finder_path_is_absolute_and_has_no_control_character, terminals_script_runners_and_unknown_apps_never_open_a_file, the_handler_lookup_refuses_a_terminal_and_opens_nothing, crates/flare-win/src/macos/workspace.rs; a_mac_folder_open_refuses_a_file_and_a_missing_path_and_shows_nothing, a_mac_folder_open_shows_the_canonical_folder_and_a_bundle_is_only_shown, a_mac_open_path_shows_a_folder_and_reveals_a_file, crates/flare-win/src/openers.rs)
- [x] Single instance: the second launch hands its folders to the first over a Unix socket in the per-user temp folder and exits; the first opens them as tabs and comes forward; another user's server is refused before a byte is written, a stalled client cannot jam it, a stopped one refuses. (macOS: a_second_launch_hands_over_and_a_first_runs, a_hand_off_server_that_is_not_trusted_refuses_the_launch, a_handoff_to_a_quitting_instance_runs_as_first, crates/flare/src/startup.rs; the single_instance tests, crates/flare-win/src/single_instance.rs)
- [x] Settings and data in `~/Library/Application Support/Flare`, the log in `~/Library/Logs/Flare`, caches in `~/Library/Caches/Flare`; a `--user-data-dir` profile keeps its own. (macOS: the_mac_folders_are_the_library_ones, crates/flare-win/src/paths.rs; the_default_mac_profile_logs_to_library_logs, crates/flare/src/startup.rs)
- [x] Process memory and the installed RAM for the diagnostics: resident set and physical footprint (`proc_pid_rusage`), `hw.memsize`; a shell's footprint counts. (macOS: no_source_means_no_breakdown_until_the_window_sets_one, crates/flare/src/memory.rs; an_idle_start_writes_nothing_else_for_three_seconds, crates/flare/src/diag_ctl.rs; this_process_has_a_resident_set_and_a_footprint, the_installed_ram_is_at_least_a_gigabyte, crates/flare-proc/src/macos.rs)
- [x] The eyedropper is the system colour sampler (`NSColorSampler`), answered in sRGB `#rrggbb`; the double-click time is the user's. (macOS: unit_components_become_the_lower_case_hex, crates/flare-win/src/eyedrop.rs; the_mac_interval_is_in_ms_and_a_strange_one_falls_back, crates/flare-win/src/dblclick.rs)
Not yet on Max's Mac (environment, not a ratchet line): Ctrl+C, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+R reaching the shell. Karabiner's "Windows: Ctrl+letter shortcuts -> Cmd" rule turns them into Cmd+letter in every app outside its terminal list, and an unbundled `flare` has no bundle id to exclude, so Ctrl+C arrives as Cmd+C (copy, or nothing) and Ctrl+T, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+A become Flare chords. Needs Max's OK for a Karabiner exclusion (a `file_paths` condition on the `flare` binary now, the bundle id once phase 6 packs `Flare.app`). Environment, not code: Flare's keys are right when the key arrives unremapped.
Still to see in CI (macOS runner, not a ratchet line): the lights centred in the row, no window drag from a tab press in the top 28 pt, the glass showing through at a see-through level, the menu bar (no second Enter Full Screen item added by AppKit, Cmd+C and Cmd+V in the find field and the Settings fields), Option typing @ on a Norwegian layout with the IME on; the eyedropper loupe picking a known colour and Escape cancelling, Finder opening a folder and revealing a file, a second `flare` from Terminal opening its folder as a tab in the running one; a window saved on a 1x screen beside a 2x one reopening there; a `.txt` opening in TextEdit while one set to open with Terminal is refused; a Mac `--e2e` run with no Dock icon and no focus change.

## macOS port, phase 4: the agent tools (2026-11-08)

The control channel and the agent tools as they hold on macOS (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-macos-port.md`,
phase 4; regression rule 1012).

- [x] The control channel is a Unix socket with the named pipe's framing, grants and names (the same FNV-1a hashes, per user and per scope), in a 0700 folder of the user's, mode 0600; the server drops a client of another uid (`getpeereid`) and the client refuses a server of another uid or, with `FLARE_CTL_PID`, any other process (`LOCAL_PEERPID`); a live server on the path fails the start through the lock it holds, even with a full backlog, a dead socket is replaced, a client of a busy server waits within its timeout while one of a dead socket fails at once (rule 1018), stop frees the name and frees a worker stuck writing to a client that never reads. (macOS: every test of crates/flare-ctl/tests/pipe.rs, and its macos::the_socket_name_is_per_user_and_per_scope, a_live_server_with_a_full_backlog_is_never_replaced, a_client_of_a_busy_server_waits_and_then_gets_in, a_client_of_a_dead_socket_fails_at_once, the_socket_name_has_the_pipe_names_known_answers, the_live_socket_is_mode_0600_in_a_0700_folder_of_this_user, the_socket_is_closed_to_other_users, the_live_server_refuses_a_client_of_another_uid, the_live_client_refuses_a_server_of_another_uid, a_folder_open_to_others_or_of_another_user_is_refused, a_dead_socket_is_replaced_and_a_file_in_the_way_is_not, stop_never_removes_a_socket_it_did_not_bind)
- [x] `flare-mcp` over the socket: a tool call reaches the server with its tab, a guessed tab token is refused, a server that is not the named process is never given the secret, mod tools are listed and announced, and the prompt hook reports a page once. (macOS: the five server tests of crates/flare-mcp/tests/stdio.rs, no longer ignored; show_html_routes_html_or_a_path_and_never_both_posix, show_image_routes_to_view_image_with_an_absolute_path_posix, crates/flare-mcp/src/lib.rs)
- [x] Every `show_*` tool, `open_page`, `close_panel`, `set_tab_badge`, `clear_tab_badge`, `set_tab_progress`, `clear_tab_progress` and `convo.state` from the agent's side over the real socket to the app's real server and the headless window. (macOS: every_show_set_tab_and_convo_tool_reaches_the_window_over_the_socket, a_caller_without_the_tabs_token_or_told_another_process_gets_nothing, crates/flare/src/appctl/tests/ctl_socket_tests.rs)
- [x] The MCP server is found beside the app by this system's name (`flare-mcp` in `Flare.app/Contents/MacOS`). (the_mcp_server_is_found_beside_the_exe_by_this_systems_name, crates/flare/src/ctl_app.rs)
- [x] Agent detection reads macOS command lines: the native `claude` and Homebrew `codex` by their program, an npm or bun install's launcher script under its interpreter (`node /opt/homebrew/bin/claude`) by its exact name, past an interpreter option's value and the `run` of deno and bun, never a folder named Claude or the desktop app's bundle; a shell that ran `exec claude` is the agent; a tab's foreground job whose parent is gone is still the tab's (rule 1016). Stands in for the Windows `.exe`, `.cmd` and `cli.js` reading, which is unchanged. (macOS: a_launcher_script_under_its_interpreter_is_the_agent_it_is_named_for, macos_command_lines_are_read_for_the_agent_they_run, an_app_bundles_own_executable_is_not_the_cli_of_its_name, a_shell_that_became_the_agent_is_the_agent, the_foreground_job_of_a_tab_is_adopted_only_when_it_is_outside_the_tree, crates/flare-agent/src/detect.rs; a_real_child_command_line_is_read_and_filtered, crates/flare-win/src/macos/procs.rs)
- [x] The port owner table through libproc (`PROC_PIDFDSOCKETINFO`), read without connecting: the dev preview chip's tree look and the Sonara owner check run on the Mac; a listener of another account is not seen (rule 1013), so an in-tree look stands only when the loopback family the tree does not hold binds free (rule 1019); a descriptor list that fills its buffer is read again. (macOS: sockets::tests, a_full_descriptor_list_is_taken_as_cut_and_asked_for_again, crates/flare-proc/src/sockets.rs; an_in_tree_answer_stands_only_when_the_unseen_family_is_shown_free, the_loopback_probe_tells_a_held_address_from_a_free_one, crates/flare-win/src/tcp_owner.rs; a_listening_tcp_socket_is_read_with_its_port_and_family, crates/flare-proc/src/lib.rs; a_childs_listener_is_in_this_processs_tree_on_macos and the tcp_owner tests, crates/flare-win/src/tcp_owner.rs; posts_the_tab_and_the_question_to_the_sonara_in_runtime_json, a_non_200_is_false_and_a_slow_server_times_out, crates/flare-agent/src/sonara.rs; sonara_is_never_called_on_the_ui_thread, crates/flare/src/agent_hub.rs)
- [x] The taskbar badge is the Dock tile's badge label ("3", "9+"), the bell's flash a critical attention request only while Flare is not the active app, and "in front" is `NSApp isActive` (rule 1014). (macOS: dock::tests, crates/flare-win/src/macos/dock.rs; a_text_badge_gets_the_count_as_the_disc_shows_it_and_a_clear_takes_it, crates/flare-win/src/taskbar.rs; dock_badge_reads_back_and_attention_is_asked_only_while_flare_is_not_active, crates/flare-win/tests/appkit.rs)
- [x] The Claude plugin on the Mac: hooks run `/bin/sh hook.sh` with the same state words and print what the parser reads, the copy lives in `~/Library/Application Support/Flare/claude-plugin-<tag>`, the plugin folder list is `:`-separated as Claude Code reads it, Claude's home (the Changes panel's transcripts, resume) is `HOME` (rule 1017). (macOS: the seven install tests of crates/flare-agent/src/plugin.rs, no longer ignored; the_posix_render_keeps_every_state_word_and_refuses_what_it_cannot_convert, every_hook_prints_what_the_parser_reads_on_macos, prints_the_state_as_valid_json_whatever_the_session_id_holds_on_macos, hook_sh_is_lf_and_static, a_platform_installs_its_own_hook_script_and_never_the_other; the_folder_list_is_split_the_way_claude_code_splits_it, crates/flare-shells/src/env.rs; claude_home_is_the_platforms_home, crates/flare-agent/src/resume.rs)
- [x] Questions as notifications go through one backend seam; on the Mac it answers "unavailable until the app is signed" up front, so the Dock bounce stands in at once with no worker and one log line a run, as when Windows refuses a toast (rule 1015). (the_backend_gets_the_request_and_its_answer_and_click_come_back, the_system_says_up_front_whether_it_can_take_a_notification; macOS: macos_says_notifications_wait_for_the_signed_app_and_shows_nothing, crates/flare-win/src/toast.rs)

Real Mac notifications through `UNUserNotificationCenter` came with port phase 8 step 2 (rule 1044, the lines under phase 8); a banner on a signed run is still to be seen by hand. Still to see in CI or on a Mac by hand (not a ratchet line): the Dock icon bouncing on a bell in a background tab and stopping when Flare comes forward, the badge count on the Dock tile.

## macOS port, phase 5: the web engine (2026-11-08)

The engine under the web panel and agent browsing on macOS: CEF, as the phase 1 verdict ruled
(`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-macos-port.md`, phase 5; regression rules 1020 to 1022). Step 1 is the engine
alone (`crates/flare-cef`); step 2 is the `WebBackend` host on top of it: the panel, downloads, mini-apps, mods and
the walls (rules 1023 to 1026). Each line names the Windows behaviour it stands in for and the macOS tests that hold it.

- [x] The engine is pinned and fetched outside the repo: CEF 156.0.14 (Chromium 156.0.8078.48) through `cef`/`cef-dll-sys` =156.2.0, macOS only, into `~/Library/Caches/flare-cef`; Windows keeps WebView2 and builds none of it. Stands in for the Evergreen WebView2 Runtime check. (macOS: the_pin_names_one_build_and_the_workspace_pins_both_crates_to_it, the_cache_is_outside_the_repo_and_the_helpers_are_the_five_cef_looks_for, crates/flare-cef/src/pin.rs)
- [x] A bundle CEF can run: the framework in `Contents/Frameworks`, the five helper apps, each entering the sandbox, signed ad hoc inside out. (macOS: the_dev_bundle_holds_the_framework_and_five_signed_helpers, crates/flare-cef/tests/engine_macos.rs; the_helpers_are_named_and_numbered_the_way_cef_looks_for_them, a_helper_plist_hides_the_helper_and_the_probe_plist_is_background_only, crates/flare-cef/src/macos/bundle.rs)
- [x] A page is drawn without a window: windowless rendering into a BGRA buffer at the device scale (983x700 CSS at 2x is 1966x1400), the page's colours where it put them; no window of the app or a helper ever on screen, the app never active, nothing native asked for. Stands in for the WebView2 controller in its child window. (macOS: a_windowless_page_paints_into_a_pixel_buffer_with_no_window_on_screen, crates/flare-cef/tests/engine_macos.rs)
- [x] The DevTools Protocol the agent speaks: `CallDevToolsProtocolMethod`'s stand-in is `SendDevToolsMessage`; the real `agent_job` drives browser_look (list, device scale, picture), click by ref, type into a ref (accents, CJK, emoji), press, scroll, wait (met and unmet) and eval (value, promise, throw), and its 14 methods (the covered-click check's three included) answer in Chromium's shapes. (macOS: the_devtools_methods_agent_job_uses_answer_in_chromiums_shapes, crates/flare-cef/tests/engine_macos.rs)
- [x] A runaway `browser_eval` (`while(true){}`, a page function that never returns, a page getter set as a trap) is stopped by `Runtime.evaluate`'s timeout, and the same page answers again at once with its state; a look on the trapped page still answers. As on Windows. (macOS: a_runaway_eval_is_stopped_by_its_timeout_and_the_page_answers_again_with_its_state, crates/flare-cef/tests/engine_macos.rs)

Step 2, the web panel (regression rules 1023 to 1026): the `WebBackend` host on CEF (`crates/flare-web/src/macos/host.rs`),
driven through the trait in a headless dev bundle by `crates/flare-web/tests/cef_macos.rs`.

- [x] The web panel opens, navigates and reports as WebView2 does: Ready, Loading and Loaded by the request's number, Url, Title, History; back, forward, reload, stop; a fragment and `history.pushState` are SameDocument moves; Back on the first page goes nowhere; a failed load carries WebView2's error status (a refused connection is CANNOT_CONNECT, 12). Stands in for the WebView2 controller's events. (macOS: panel.open, panel.history, panel.same-document, panel.failed-load in the_web_panel_on_cef_navigates_draws_takes_input_and_holds_its_walls_with_no_window, crates/flare-web/tests/cef_macos.rs; a_chromium_error_reads_as_the_webview2_status_the_failure_notice_knows, crates/flare-cef/src/status.rs; devtools_events_become_new_documents_same_document_moves_and_binding_calls, crates/flare-cef/src/macos/view.rs)
- [x] The page is a picture in the panel's box at the panel's device pixels, a `<select>` list a painted layer of it, a still its PNG; Windows' child window stands in. (macOS: panel.frame, panel.still, panel.select-list; an_off_screen_page_is_drawn_in_its_box_takes_the_input_there_and_a_press_stops_the_agent, crates/flare/src/appctl/tests/web_tests.rs; an_open_select_list_is_laid_over_the_page_where_chromium_put_it, a_premultiplied_pixel_comes_out_straight_and_a_still_is_a_png, crates/flare-cef/src/macos/view.rs)
- [x] Input over the box reaches the page as the person's: a click (trusted, where it was made), the wheel, keys (ASCII, an accent, Backspace), the input method's text, focus in and out; a chord the app takes (Cmd+T) goes to the window and never to the page; a press or a typed key while an agent works ends its call (the stop-on-input monitor). (macOS: panel.pointer, panel.wheel, panel.keys-and-ime, panel.key-filter, panel.focus; an_off_screen_page_is_drawn_in_its_box_takes_the_input_there_and_a_press_stops_the_agent; on_a_mac_the_backends_ctrl_is_cmd_and_the_app_takes_its_cmd_chords, crates/flare/src/web_ctl.rs; slints_keys_become_the_keys_chromium_knows, crates/flare/src/web_live.rs; a_key_that_types_nothing_carries_appkits_character, on_a_mac_cmd_is_the_key_the_chord_table_reads_as_ctrl, crates/flare-web/src/macos/host.rs)
- [x] Zoom is the browser's own (150 % is Chromium's level 2.22). (macOS: panel.zoom; a_zoom_factor_is_chromiums_level, crates/flare-cef/src/status.rs)
- [x] Nothing native and nothing without a yes: script dialogs answered at once, camera, mic, location, notifications and clipboard read denied with no prompt, `mailto:` refused and `file:` never loaded, a new window loads in the same view, a download held until the person says yes (nothing saved on a no; a yes saves it in Downloads, never over a file). (macOS: panel.dialogs, panel.permissions, panel.external-schemes, panel.new-window-same-view, panel.downloads; a_download_is_named_by_its_last_part_and_never_overwrites, crates/flare-cef/src/status.rs; the_web_panel_goes_to_web_addresses_and_its_frames_anywhere, crates/flare-web/src/walls.rs)
- [x] DevTools calls through the backend answer with Chromium's result, a script with `ExecuteScript`'s JSON, and a page whose process ends is Gone. (macOS: panel.cdp, panel.gone; a_script_answer_reads_as_execute_scripts_json, crates/flare-web/src/macos/host.rs)

Accepted gap (rule 1033, not a ratchet line): the web panel's profile and each mod's and plugin's are kept on disk with
Chromium's mock keychain, whose key is public, so their cookies and storage are protected only by the files'
permissions (Windows uses DPAPI). Since phase 8 step 2 this is true of every build but the Developer ID app's real
window, which keeps the key in a keychain item of its own (rule 1045).

Not yet on the Mac (the next steps of phase 5, not ratchet lines): running the real Flare window with CEF needs Flare
itself inside an app bundle with the framework and helpers (`Flare.app`, phase 8; a `cargo run` says the engine is not
in its bundle), so the panel in the live window, the plugin bars' pictures under the terminal (their views run, the bar
box does not draw them yet), the agent's cursor and border over the page, and the window's own e2e scenarios
(`tools/e2e` web, mini-app, live preview, agent web, fill) wait for the bundle and the CI macOS runner. The web panel's
context menu (Windows shows the browser's) is not drawn yet: it is emptied and never shown natively. The input method's
composition in the page needs Slint to hand over preedit text (the box gets the committed text as keys). Diagrams and
live preview are mini-apps and ride on the mini-app path; a diagram's Mermaid run is not checked on CEF yet. The
WebView2 password autosave and form autofill have no CEF Alloy counterpart (`ControllerProbe::autofill` is false).
A frame's contents are not in the numbered list (the agent uses a frame by point); on Windows the same Chromium
call gives the root frame's tree, but a Windows look of the agent page has not been diffed with the Mac one yet. Waits for the Developer ID (phase 8): the signed
bundle, the hardened runtime and its entitlements, notarization, camera and microphone prompts for programs run in
the terminal (a page's camera and microphone are refused on both platforms: WebView2's handler and CEF's deny them).
The keychain item for the profiles on disk is phase 8 step 2 (rule 1045).

## macOS port, phase 8: shipping the Mac app (2026-11-08)

Step 1, `Flare.app` (regression rules 1036 to 1039). Windows ships through Velopack (`tools/release/pack.ps1`); the Mac
ships an app bundle built by `tools/release/mac/make-app.sh` (`flare-mac-app`, `flare_cef::macos::{release, bundle,
sign}`), under `target/mac-release`.

  workspace version, the developer-tools category, the microphone's reason; `Contents/MacOS/Flare` and `flare-mcp`, the
  CEF framework and its five helpers, `Flare.icns` from `build/icon-source.png`, the Claude plugin, the licenses and
  CEF's credits in `Resources`. Stands in for the Velopack staging folder. (macOS:

  crates/flare-agent/src/plugin.rs)
- [x] Signing inside out with the Developer ID, hardened runtime, secure timestamp, each program's entitlements
  (Chromium 156's, plus a terminal's camera, microphone and Apple Events keys with their usage strings), and every
  Mach-O checked for the team, a Developer ID Application signer and exactly its role's keys; no `codesign` with the
  identity unless the key cannot raise a prompt (rules 1036, 1038, 1040 to 1042). Stands in for Authenticode (not done on
  Windows yet). (macOS: each_program_carries_chromiums_keys_for_it_plus_the_terminals_and_never_the_broad_ones,

Not yet (the next steps of phase 8, not ratchet lines): the notarized and stapled app and DMG with `spctl` saying
"source=Notarized Developer ID". The script does it (`make-app.sh`, `macos-app.yml`), but on Max's Mac
`flare-mac-app check-key` refuses: the Developer ID key's partition list is only `apple-tool:`, so `codesign` would
put a keychain prompt on the screen (rule 1040). It runs once Max has run `security set-key-partition-list -S
apple-tool:,apple: -s ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db` himself. Then: the entitlements proved by the engine
probes under the hardened runtime (`FLARE_TEST_SIGN_IDENTITY` and `FLARE_TEST_SIGN_KEYCHAIN`, `cargo nextest run -p
flare-cef -p flare-web`), with WebGL on SwiftShader (`--use-angle=swiftshader`) for the GPU helper's JIT key;
Velopack osx-arm64 updates with a whole-bundle swap, signed and notarized after `vpk pack` and checked on the bundle
inside the update package (rule 1043): built in step 3 below; the release notes and the Mac build come in
`macos-app.yml` (step 3), not in `release.yml`.
